### Materials from German-language sources


Damian Kruczkowski

#### Łazińsk — the village's history spans over 280 years[^93]

Łazińsk is a village formerly in Konin county, in which a predominantly German population lived. Heading southwest from the county town of Konin on the Warta, one arrived after a while at the gmina of Drommin (Trąbczyn), to which — besides the village of Michalinów — belonged the settlement of Łazińsk, inhabited almost exclusively by Germans. Until 1945 some 170 German families still lived there.

It was the year 1718 when 14 German families arrived from Silesia, led by their village headman, and from the owner of the Trąbczyn landed estate they received a large area of forest in order to bring it under cultivation. From hard pioneering work there arose the village of Łazińsk "from the green root" [*aus grüner Wurzel*].

The families living here were named Kuhn, Merk, Schönfisch, Haupt. These surnames were still encountered in this area until 1945, and can be found many a time on the ancestor table of the Weber/Rhytthammer family.

In the year 1968 the German settlement could have celebrated the 250th anniversary of its existence, had history taken a different course.

To the left beside the kantorate school, on a gently rising hill, lay a richly wooded cemetery in which our ancestors laid the first grave in the year 1727. A most ancient resting place, whose weathered and rotted gravestones of wood and iron existed still until the year 1945.



[^93]: Source: Note in: Der Heimatbote, 8/81-3 Kartoteka Wartheland (the Warta Land), Poland. Translated by Jacek Kin.

<!-- s. 290 [PDF 290] -->

The colonists built their own school, at the same time a house of prayer, quite early on, and before long Łazińsk, numbering 150 inhabitants, became the largest kantorate in the later gmina of Zagórów (Hinterberg).

The cantor in Łazińsk not only taught, but also celebrated the Sunday services. Until 1933 instruction and services took place in the old building. Under the direction of the teacher and cantor of the time, [Gustav] Mielke, a new building was erected which served as school and house of prayer. The Polish authorities allowed him to carry on his work for four years, until he was transferred to Galicia on account of his over-zealousness in cultivating Germandom.

As a result of the German attack in September 1939 and the victory of the German Wehrmacht over the Polish army, the Polish state ceased to exist for the second time after twenty years of existence. Among its consequences were, among other things, expulsions of population, which, however, did not touch Łazińsk directly. The courageous attitude of the Polish and German inhabitants prevented the plundering of the German farms by the Poles as well as the expulsion of the Polish farmers from their holdings. In that time more pupils attended the school in Łazińsk than ever before. Teacher Mielke, too, taught again at this school from the year 1940. Apart from him, his sister and two young teachers from Thuringia taught there. The school was now attended by 153 children. More Germans lived in the vicinity than before. They were Germans who had arrived from the Reich [Reichsdeutsche], as well as resettlers from Volhynia and from other areas.

At the end of the war, in the year 1945, the inhabitants of Łazińsk met the same fate as all Germans: the loss of their land, flight, expulsion, deportation, dispersal. It rarely happens that the former neighbors meet anywhere at all and share their memories.

In contrast to the former inhabitants of Łazińsk, the inhabitants of the neighboring Orlina maintain contact to this very day. Here it is Mrs. Marga Todt, a former teacher at the school in Orlina, who looks after keeping the bond alive. Every year at the beginning of May she organizes the "Orlina Meetings" in her home in Mühlheim an der Ruhr.

<!-- s. 291 [PDF 291] -->

#### Population and administration

1815-1918 the village belongs to the Russian partition (Congress Poland)

1921 Official name: Łazińska Kolonia

- gmina Trąbczyn, Słupca county,
- with 900 inhabitants,
- of whom 571 of German nationality — 757 of Evangelical denomination,
- 144 of Polish nationality — 138 of Catholic denomination, 5 of Jewish nationality

1933 Łazińska Kolonia, gmina Trąbczyn, Konin county

- post office, Evangelical parish of Zagórów,
- District Court (Amtsgericht) of Słupca,
- Catholic parish of Trąbczyn

1940-1945 Łazińsk, administrative district (Amtsbezirk) and post office of Drommin (Trąbczyn), Konin county, government region of Inowrocław (Hohensalza)

\*

At the present time the former Łazińsk, whose name was often subject to various "modifications", exists within the gmina of Zagórów, Słupca county, in the form of two villages.  
Łazińsk Pierwszy – the village has 159 inhabitants.  
Łazińsk Drugi – the village has 82 inhabitants[^94].



[^94]: Data of the Office of the Town and Municipality of Zagórów as of 23.10.2023.

<!-- s. 292 [PDF 292] -->

### The Parish of Zagórów[^95]

Compiled by Günter Hennings[^95]

The settlement of the Zagórów area by Evangelical colonists began as early as the middle of the 18th century. Łazińsk became the largest kantorate of the later gmina and comprised 150 inhabitants. Besides Łazińsk, the following were also established: Tarszewo (Friedrichsfelde), Chruściki (Adelshof), Michalinów (Emilienheim), Olchowo (Sophiental), Marly Las (Ludwigslust), Anielewo (Ingelfingen) and Augustynów (Augustinenhof). Around the year 1800 clothmakers also settled in Zagórów.

The immigrants remained at first under the care of pastors from Grodziec, Konin and Żychlin.

On 29 July 1840 the Evangelical Christians resolved to establish a filial in Zagórów and to elect a church college. However, the final establishment of the filial did not take place until 1843. Services were at first held in rented premises, and from the year 1850 in the house at 54 Konińska Street. The filial was tended by Pastor Orive. In the year 1856 Zagórów became independent, and Pastor Winkler became the first local incumbent. A year later the parish acquired two small buildings as a parsonage. After only four years of activity Pastor Winkler left the parish, and his successors Eugen Biermann (1860-1865), Karl Eduard Erdmann (1876-1878) and Bernard Erdmann (1898-1903) served here only briefly as well.

Likewise during the First World War the parish administrator Haack from Germany was here only three years. During vacancies the parish in Zagórów 


[^95]: Source: Note in: Der Heimatbote, 8/81-3. Kartoteka Wartheland. Translated by Jacek Kin. Günter Hennings – the nephew of Eugenie Plitt, née Schmechel, born in Germany, lives in Küsten in the district of Lüchow-Dannenberg in Lower Saxony on the Elbe [supplemented by the translator].

<!-- s. 293 [PDF 293] -->
 was administered first by the pastor from Stawiszyn (1865-1876), then from Grodziec and from Konin.

During the tenure of Karl Eduard Erdmann, on 8 May 1878 the foundation stone was laid for the construction of the Evangelical church in Zagórów.

The construction of the church lasted four years and cost over 13,000 rubles. During Advent of the year 1882 the consecration took place. In the year 1883 the organ was ordered, and in 1884 two bells. The contribution of Pastor Erdmann, who from the year 1887 cared for the parish from Grodziec and died in the year 1892, was honored with a marble memorial tablet in the church. In the year 1898 the house of prayer in Adelshof was built, and in the year 1917, after four years of construction, the kantorate and school building in Zagórów was completed.

As the last pastor, Alexander Gross held the office in the years 1923-1945, born on 5.5.1898 in Łódź, who was closely bound to the Christian parish.

In his time the parish acquired a new bell in place of the bell requisitioned in the First World War, and a clock for the tower, which was funded by the Krüger couple in the year 1927.

The kantortes of Łazińsk and Ciemierów received back through the courts, in the year 1935, the property that had been taken from them. In the year 1938 the parish house was consecrated, which cost 40,000 złotys. In the year 1945 Pastor Gross had to leave Zagórów, together with several hundred other Germans, and was deported to Stalino. There he worked in a quarry and in a lime works. He fell ill and died in the middle of November 1945 in the infirmary in Finolny.

After the war Zagórów was for a long time under the care of Pastor Świtalski, today Bensheim.

Around the year 1980 services were held here once a month by Pastor Andrzej Mendrok from Konin. In this period Ewald Riedel lived in the parish house. He was the church warden (Kirchenvorsteher), at the same time the sexton, and that at the age of 80.

<!-- s. 294 [PDF 294] -->

### Settlement history of the Zagórów-Trąbczyn region

#### Settlement history of the Greater Zagórów-Trąbczyn area (Siedlungsgeschichte Großraum Zagorow-Trabczyn)[^96]

#### ZAGÓRÓW (1943-1945 HINTERBERG)

- Year 2014 – Zagorow is a town and county seat in Powiat Slupecki, Wojewodschaft Großpolen in Poland.  
  The year 2014 – Zagórów is a town in Słupca county, in the Greater Poland Voivodeship.
- The town lies on the River Warthe about 30 km west of Konin. Until the Second World War the town was surrounded by many small German settlements.  
  The town is situated on the Warta river, about 30 km west of Konin. Until the Second World War the town was surrounded by many small German settlements.
- In the year 1145 German Cistercian monks founded the monastery of Lad (Lond) on the Warthe. Around this monastery the monks promoted the establishment of small settlements, since they received dues from the peasants. In 1553 the German monks left the territory and fled with the charters to the monastery of Heinrichau in Silesia, because a Polish government took over in Lad.  
  In the year 1145 the Cistercians founded a monastery in Ląd on the Warta. The monks supported the founding of small settlements around this monastery, since they received tributes from the peasants. In the year 1553 the German monks left this area and moved, together with the documents, to the Heinrichau monastery in Silesia, because a Polish administration came to power in Ląd.

### Protocol of the meeting of the communal/parish assembly of the Cantors' Council of Łazińsk, held on 26 November 1928

The agenda included:

Construction of a small church

Acquisition of funds for the construction

Election of the building committee

1\) After long deliberation the undersigned resolved to build a small church on the territory of the Cantors' Council area.

2\) The collection of funds for this construction was approved in the form of a tax amounting to 7 złotych per day and 10 zł per citizen.

3\) The building committee consists of the following gentlemen, whose names are: Magnat Schuller, Schuch Gustaw, Brunsch Lenard, August Grunwald, Gnater Seer, Wilhelm Moritz, August Schonfisch, Osdaldt Mirundt, Gustaw Mielke, teacher. It was then decided that, when purchasing material for the construction, not only the members of the committee would be present, but also two or three members of the communal/parish community.

Further steps will be decided at special meetings.

Chairman: August Grunwald

Deputy: Schuch Gustav

Minutes taker: Gottlieb Karinnger Haar

### Michalinów Trąbczyński, Łazińsk, Huta Trąbczyńska. Inhabitants List

The village of Michalinów Trąbczyński lies to the south of Trąbczyn and is recorded in the Catholic church register of Trąbczyn as a locality already around the year 1800. Even then many German families lived there. This Michalinów is often confused by mistake with the village bearing the same name, Michalinów (Emilienheim), and causes many problems in the search for ancestors from the early years before the year 1843, because the two localities belonged to two different Catholic churches.

For this reason the names carry additional designations: Michalinów Trąbczyński, the one discussed here, and Michalinów Oleśnicki (= Emilienheim).

Below is a list of the former inhabitants of the locality, state as of the year 1945, which was compiled by Mr. Leonard Kuske of 28857 Syke. Thanks are also due to Mrs. Regina Schiewe for her general help in assembling the facts.

Michalinów Trąbczyński: Ludwig Sidow, the Zache family, Leonard Kuske (of the Łazy precinct), Karl & Daniel Schulz, the Buhlmann family, the Stark family, the Zellmer family, the Giebel family, Hulda Lehmann, Leonard Lehmann, Heinrich Gliege, the Weidner family, Walter Schultz, Daniel Krüger, the Kuck family, Roman Stark, Rafael Stark, Reinhold Marschal, Rudolf Gliege, Christian Gliege, Robert Zellmer, Emil Welk, Wypychowski (Polish family)[^97], Rudolf Liepelt, Eduard Schulz, School & House of Prayer, Julius Winterfeld, the Pede family, Julius Schneider, Marciniak (Polish family), Urbaniak (Polish family), the Schurak family, Julius Schur, Ewald Haupt, Walter

### Reproduction Officer

We are publishing a document discovered in the kantorate in Łazińsk, a copy of which we reproduce in full. It is a rare example of a forgery produced by an underground cell specializing in distributing among the Germans fictitious, misleading information and directives.

Header: Reichsstelle für Familienzuwach. (Reich Office for Family Increase) Berlin 15.5.1943. The matter "concerns" a citizen of the Reich. One must add, however, that the German offices caring for the purity of the race carried out a kind of selection among the slave laborers directed to work in Germany, qualifying the chosen men as so-called race breeders.



Photo: Aneta Węcławiak

<!-- s. 308 [PDF 308] -->

> Reichsstelle für Familienzuwach  
> Reich Office for Family Increase  
> Hauptstelle Berlin  
> Head Office Berlin Berlin, 15.5.1943  
> Concerns: The fight against ruin (of the nation)  
> File reference I.A.4711
>
> Dear Sir!
>
> Since, as a result of the war, a certain number of men have fallen, there now rests upon those still living the duty of attending to the women and girls, so that the birth rate may be considerably raised.
>
> Henceforth you are deemed a person of particular productivity, and we resolve accordingly to confer upon you the honorary office of fulfilling the German duty. It is pointed out that your wife has no right to divorce on account of this duty of yours, since every woman must accept this small inconvenience as a consequence of the war. There is assigned to you a district, which will be specified more closely, comprising 9 women and 18 girls. If you should turn out to be incapable of performing the task assigned to you, you are then obliged to report this within 3 days, quoting the file reference mentioned above, and naming an efficient man as your deputy. If, however, you are able to take over, in addition to your district, one more district, you will then be appointed a reproduction officer and will receive, besides the reproduction premium, also the Order for Copulation, First Class, with red ribbon. You will receive the list of the ladies whom you are to visit shortly. Your blessed activity you will please begin at once, and the success, after 9 months, is to be reported here on a form which will be sent separately. In order to preserve your efficiency, the offices responsible for nutrition have been instructed to issue to the gentlemen concerned additional food cards for persons performing heavy work, and in the case of 2 districts, for persons performing very heavy work, and you are therefore asked to contact the nutrition office responsible for you.
>
> Reich Office for Population Increase  
> signed: Hüpfauf

*Translated by Jacek Kin*

### FRYDHOF is not just a cemetery.


Damian Kruczkowski

#### The idea, local heritage, the fight for memory: on the association's activities

How did it come about that we founded an association, and what were the beginnings of the Stowarzyszenie FRYDHOF? We always have a bit of trouble answering that question, because we simply are. It happened. And it is. Something in the nature of an accord of thoughts, views, desires and actions must surely have come to pass. Above all, a desire to work to save things from oblivion. That, too, is how we most often present it. First there was a community of views, actions and motives among individuals acting on their own; then a group came together; and finally that group brought into being an association: the Wielkopolskie Stowarzyszenie na rzecz Ratowania Pamięci FRYDHOF. But before we begin to describe the history of the association itself, it is worth saying something about the motives by which the Stowarzyszenie FRYDHOF is guided. It is precisely this that we speak of when we answer the question: why do you do it? What unites us, namely, is a deep refusal to accept transience and forgetting, as well as the effacement and devastation of the last traces left behind by people – whole social groups – who long ago departed into history.

That refusal, that incomprehension of certain human behaviours leading to the effacement of traces and the denial of historical facts, and even a certain kind of anger directed at the process of forgetting, meant that in several places of so-called eastern Wielkopolska there appeared two groups of enthusiasts to whom the historical heritage of the Hauländer settlers became dear – putting it in confessional terms, the historical heritage of the Evangelicals of eastern Wielkopolska. In many localities formerly inhabited by the settlers, this heritage is already only the cemeteries, which are the last traces of these people's presence.

<!-- s. 312 [PDF 312] -->

From the post-war years, that is from 1945, often up to the present day, the Hauländer cemeteries – or, to put it another way, the Evangelical cemeteries – were the target of attacks by the local population, the local authorities, and eventually plain hooliganism. It is not for us to judge the motives of human actions we do not understand, not for us to mete out punishment, so we usually do not wonder why it happened that way. What we do wonder is what we can do, and we set to work. For several years the two groups mentioned travelled across the territory of the former Konin voivodeship, explored Hauländer settlements, and found and discovered Evangelical cemeteries independently of each other. In the area of Koło and the Koło and Turek counties, the enthusiasts of the heritage of the Hauländers chiefly gathered around Urszula Karolczak, while in Konin and the Konin and Słupca counties it was the writer of these words – Damian Kruczkowski. We were not formal leaders of these groups. Rather it was that, knowing of our interests, people who in some way had at heart the preservation of the material traces of the former inhabitants of the lands on which we worked would turn to us for help, and often simply formed ad hoc groups together with us, which tidied up and restored successive cemeteries, thereby restoring the memory of the Evangelical inhabitants of a given locality, and thus restoring the cemetery to the local communities. These two centres of the fight for memory existed, as I have said, independently of each other for several years, trying with lesser or greater success to preserve as much as possible of the historical heritage of the region of the former Konin voivodeship.

Today probably none of us remembers exactly when these two groups met each other, but it is certain that together we worked through the whole of 2016, acting as an informal group of "grobersi," as we were somewhat ironically called. We worked entirely on a voluntary basis, as we continue to do, often buying the necessary equipment, fuel and materials with our own money. In fact we did everything at our own expense, only occasionally receiving some small assistance from local communities. Now no longer two groups but one larger one grew steadily, which 
<!-- s. 313 [PDF 313] -->
 each time made it possible to broaden the scope of the works. It was no longer only clearing up, hauling away fallen trees and branches, cutting out shrubs and mowing the grass that lay within the bounds of our capabilities; thanks to the numbers and strength of our group it also became possible to lift the heavy stone blocks scattered about, of which the gravestones had been built, and to glue together the ones that had been smashed. More and more often we thought about formally constituting the group, so that, being more credible and transparent, we could apply for grants and subsidies for our activity. On this matter we were unanimous. And our group has been made up, from the very beginning until today, of various people representing almost the entire cross-section of Polish society.

From the very beginning of the functioning of both groups, and later as one, we have cooperated closely with the Evangelical-Augsburg parish of the Holy Spirit in Konin, under whose jurisdiction are now found all the Evangelical cemeteries of the area of the former Konin voivodeship, and with the Evangelical parishes in Koło, Turek and Zagórów. Pastor Waldemar Wunsz was engaged in our activities from the beginning, contributing greatly to them and working with us. The pastor is now a member of the association's board and belongs to the group of founding members. At the end of 2016 we jointly made the decision to create an association, which should make many matters easier for us, not least those connected with seeking financial resources or with obtaining the necessary consents and permits for successive works.

On 13 January 2017 we were entered in the KRS, and from that date the formal period of the existence of the Stowarzyszenie FRYDHOF is counted. Urszula Karolczak became president of the association, Damian Kruczkowski deputy, Radosław Grzelak secretary, Przemysław Tomczyk treasurer, and Waldemar Wunsz a member of the board, and this composition of the board remains to this day. Our activity is focused mainly on Evangelical cemeteries, since there are the most of them – more than 200. It is on the Evangelical cemeteries, such as those in Zagórów itself and its close vicinity within the municipality, that the most has been preserved, to speak in scholarly terms, of the above-ground layer of the cemetery, that is the gravestones, which we try to save. For this reason too we looked for a name that would in a way tie our activities to the heritage we protect 
<!-- s. 314 [PDF 314] -->
 and its history, indicating the main current of our interests. After many attempts and fittings, the decision was that the association received the name Wielkopolskie Stowarzyszenie na rzecz Ratowania Pamięci FRYDHOF. The word "frydhof" is simply a Polonised version of the German word *friedhof*, meaning cemetery, which can be found in the dialects of several regions of Poland, mainly Upper Silesia and Pomerania. Many older people in those areas to this day call a cemetery precisely that. This name defines the aim of our activities and alludes to the historical multiculturalism of the region and the country. The seat of the Stowarzyszenie FRYDHOF is Konin in the Wielkopolska voivodeship. The territory of the association's activity is the whole of Poland. We have already worked in Pomerania and Lower Silesia, and with lectures we have been to almost every part of the country.

The association is a very diverse group, in which various professions and milieus are represented. Above all, however, it brings together people engaged in the protection of monuments and cultural heritage, with particular attention to the areas of eastern Wielkopolska and the Evangelical heritage. In our association it is above all people for whom their little homelands, their local history, multiculturalism and its heritage, and especially the areas of the sacrum, including cemeteries, historic architecture and archival collections, are the object of particular care and of scholarly, research, artistic and social activity. The association is made up of people who do not accept the removal, from the pages of history, from local spaces or even from social discourse, of the traces of the presence of those who have long been gone and who therefore can no longer stand in defence of the heritage they have left to us, the living. As it happens, in the case of the Hauländer settlers this heritage and what they left behind is strikingly visible. But so as not to diminish the merits of other social or confessional groups, it should be said openly that without the work of generations of people who were before us, people of many nationalities and many confessions, many of those places in which today all of us – without exception – live would be unfit for habitation, and even more of those places simply would not exist today. Every time 
<!-- s. 315 [PDF 315] -->
 we begin work at the next cemetery, we try to involve local communities in these activities. First of all, we do this because the local authorities should always be asked for permission, but also because if the local community joins in such work, then besides learning something about the place where they live, they will respect their own work more and will not allow further devastation of the cemetery grounds. In many cases this is exactly what happens. It is the local residents nearby who then keep watch so that the cemetery does not overgrow anew with grass and bushes, so that no one destroys the gravestones.

It is true that there are also places where neither the local authorities nor the residents nearby ever supported our activities, where no one ever appeared to help. In the history of the Stowarzyszenie FRYDHOF, but above all already in the history of the saving of the heritage of the Evangelicals of the Zagórów municipality, there is inscribed the municipal councillor Jan Kloc. A man of tireless energy, incredible passion and human goodness. It is chiefly thanks to him that the Evangelical-Augsburg cemetery in Michalinów Trąbczyński looks today like a cemetery. We began the work together: FRYDHOF, the Towarzystwo Przyjaciół Ziemi Zagórowskiej and Jan Kloc. But it was Mr Jan who completed the work of the renovation of this cemetery. One must not forget one more figure for whom heritage – in this case the heritage of ancestors – is not a matter of indifference. That figure is Mr Erich Kuhn, born on the Łazińsk in the Zagórów municipality, living there until the 1950s. When, in the difficult post-war times, the Evangelicals had to leave the People's Poland, Mr Erich's family and he himself left as well. Yet he never forgot his little homeland, and for more than twenty years now, every summer Erich Kuhn gathers a group of former inhabitants of the Zagórów municipality of the Evangelical-Augsburg confession, who live in the territory of the Evangelical-Augsburg parish in Detmold in Germany, and they come to Zagórów, where a Polish-German church service is then held. When, thanks among other things to the activities of the Stowarzyszenie FRYDHOF, the Towarzystwo Przyjaciół Ziemi Zagórowskiej and Mr Jan Kloc, it became possible to enter the Evangelical cemeteries on which lie 
<!-- s. 316 [PDF 316] -->



The Evangelical cemetery in Michalinów Trąbczyński.


Photo: Aneta Węcławiak



The Evangelical cemetery in Huta Łazińska.


Photo: Aneta Węcławiak



The Evangelical cemetery in Zagórów.


Photo: Aneta Węcławiak

<!-- s. 317 [PDF 317] -->



"And take this cross…".


Photo: from the FRYDHOF archive



Clean-up work at the cemetery at Łazińsk Drugi, 25 February 2017. FRYDHOF, Towarzystwo Przyjaciół Ziemi Zagórowskiej, Zagórów – Miejsce z Historią. In the front row, third from the left, Jan Kloc – the caretaker of the Evangelical cemeteries in the Zagórów municipality.

<!-- s. 318 [PDF 318] -->
 Mr Kuhn's ancestors, without having to force one's way through a thicket of bushes, dry branches and rubbish, it was precisely Erich Kuhn who initiated a collection of funds for placing, on three cemeteries important to him, memorial stones commemorating the former inhabitants of those villages resting there. Thanks to his initiative, in August 2018 stones with inscriptions in Polish and German stating that these are Evangelical cemeteries, where the inhabitants of these lands have long rested, were erected in Michalinów Trąbczyński, Łazińsk Pierwszy and Łazińsk Huta. The greatest and most welcome support is rendered to us precisely by the units of the Volunteer Fire Brigades, which have a numerous workforce marked by physical vigour, but often also heavy equipment so very necessary in many cases. Some stone or concrete blocks weigh several hundred kilograms, and to restore them to their former place truly enormous strength is needed. Without the support of the firefighters, on many cemeteries what we managed to accomplish together would have been impossible. The local fire brigades are truly a help beyond all measure. It has not yet happened that firefighters, when asked – as long as they have no other duties – did not hurry to help.


It also gladdens one – and this should be emphasised – that despite the daily rush, the municipal authorities very often find the time to bend over the problem of caring for a disused Evangelical cemetery and, within their means, take action aimed at protecting such a place. In many cases our work became the efficient cause of actions that the municipality undertook after us. An example here may be the Krzymów municipality, with the wójt Danuta Mazur at its head, who joined in the work of the association while still a municipal councillor and has continued it steadily. But the Zagórów municipality and its representatives also have considerable merits in this field. It also happens that when we have done our work, restored the local cemetery to the memory and awareness of the local community, then in a given locality an informal group of inhabitants forms who become continuators of our work and also try, not infrequently at their own expense, to look after the cemetery, 
<!-- s. 319 [PDF 319] -->
 to protect it and care for it. The activities of the Stowarzyszenie FRYDHOF are not, as is known, limited to the area of Konin, Koło and their surroundings. At the invitation of the Fundacja Pałac Stolec and the owners of the palace there, in April 2017 we tidied up, together with the inhabitants of the village of Stolec in the West Pomeranian voivodeship, the local Evangelical cemetery. In the course of the work we managed to uncover and restore a dozen or so cast-iron gravestone crosses.

Twice already, in 2017 and 2020, we took part in renovation work at the Evangelical cemetery in Gostków near Wałbrzych. We appeared there at the invitation of the owners of the cemetery, a mother and daughter. Mrs Halina Bryk (who, by the way, trace their lineage from Ląd) and Angelika Babula, two fantastic women, several years ago entered the tender announced by their municipality and bought the cemetery grounds so that a potential buyer would not raze them to the ground. The women established the Fundacja Anna z Gostkowa, which is restoring to its former glory the resting place of the Evangelical inhabitants of Gostków and its surroundings. In 2019 representatives of the Fundacja Anna came to support our activities at the Evangelical cemetery in Święcie in the Rychwał municipality. We began a cooperation with the Towarzystwo Kulturalne TEODOR from Zaniemyśl, which strives for the preservation and renovation of the local Evangelical cemetery. Overall care was taken by the voivodeship conservator of monuments from Poznań.

The activities of the Stowarzyszenie FRYDHOF are not limited solely to physical work at cemeteries; one of our missions is also education. We try to respond to invitations from local communities or groups interested in our work. Behind us are many meetings in schools in Konin, the Konin county, Koło, the Koło county, the Słupca county – among others in Zagórów. I give lectures regularly on history as part of the Third Age University which operates at the Państwowa Wyższa Szkoła Zawodowa in Konin, in the course of which I often speak about the history of the Evangelicals. In 2017 and 2018 we had the pleasure of speaking about our activities at the Centrum Dialogu im. Marka Edelmana in Łódź during the first and second editions of the conference "Cemeteries – the Silent 
<!-- s. 320 [PDF 320] -->
 Witnesses of History. Evangelical Cemeteries of the Łódź Voivodeship: History, Inventory, Renovation". In 2018, as part of the Poznań Night of Sanctuaries, we were also co-organisers of the conference "I, the Heir! I, the Heiress! – Forgotten Necropolises".

We always appear where our knowledge and experience can be helpful, where we can inspire and motivate work for the saving of memory. The Stowarzyszenie FRYDHOF also has two publications to its credit: the first, published in 2018 at its own expense, "We Restore the Memory of the Hauländers in the Warta Valley," and the second, published in 2019 with the participation of funds from the Fundacja Santander, "Hauländers around Konin". In 2017, in turn, FRYDHOF organised a promotional meeting for the diaries of Rabbi Jehoszua Mosze Aronson, "The Scroll of the House of Bondage," published by the Muzeum Okręgowe in Konin, a publication over which the late and much-missed dr Łucja Pawlicka-Nowak, who died in 2020, worked for years. The association does not focus solely on Evangelical cemeteries. We have already cooperated three times in tidying up the Jewish cemetery in Rychwał. In this action we assisted the initiators of the undertaking: the Towarzystwo Przyjaciół Ziemi Zagórowskiej, the Towarzystwo Drugie Pokolenie-Potomkowie Ocalałych z Holokaustu and the Gmina Żydowska w Poznaniu. In the book *Poniemieckie* by Karolina Kuszyk, published by Wydawnictwo Czarne in 2020 and gathering excellent reviews, one can find a mention of the work of our association. Although the object of our particular care is the Evangelical heritage, we also undertake actions at Catholic cemeteries. In 2019 we managed to restore the forgotten and crumbling tomb located at the cemetery in Konin-Morzysław, in which rest participants in the struggles for Poland's independence: the Wielkopolska insurgent Major Stanisław Zieliński, his wife, a Home Army courier, Genowefa Zielińska, and her brother-in-law Major Stanisław Bobola, a member of the Home Army, participant in the First and Second World War, decorated with the Cross of Merit and the order of Virtuti Militari. Funds from the next collection in the past year made it possible to erect at the cemetery in Morzysław a new gravestone for Klara 
<!-- s. 321 [PDF 321] -->
 née Ebel Podgajecka, the heiress of the Morzysław estate. In May 2020, at the initiative of the Stowarzyszenie FRYDHOF, a memorial gravestone was erected at the cemetery in Konin-Gosławice for Marian and Albina Bekker, the parents of Mieczysław Grzegorz Bekker, the creator of the lunar rover. Three years of intensive activity have brought tangible results. We count on the engagement and the willingness to work not passing, and on the Stowarzyszenie FRYDHOF being able to act for many more years. We take hope from the fact that there are ever more of us enthusiasts of this kind. We are scattered across the whole of Poland, fighting for the future of the past, for the shadows of people, so as to render them the homage due to them.

<!-- s. 322 [PDF 322] -->

<!-- s. 323 [PDF 323] -->

### BROKEN LINK

#### Protestants in the parish of Zagórów

Summary

The small town of Zagórów, situated in eastern Wielkopolska on the southern rise of the Warta valley, and the surrounding settlements and villages were inhabited for centuries by Poles, Jews and Germans. A symbolic expression of this multicultural region were the three Zagórów houses of worship: the Church of Saints Peter and Paul on the Small Market Square, the synagogue on Średnia Street and the Evangelical-Augsburg Church on Konińska Street. The horrors of the Second World War brutally destroyed this world.

In recent years, numerous works and publications have appeared on the history and the life of the Poles and Jews living in this region. But the history of the town and the parish of Zagórów still remains incomplete. It is extremely difficult to gain access to narratives and documents from the lives of the Protestants who lived in Zagórów and the surrounding villages – for the most part Germans – since few records exist and eyewitnesses can hardly be interviewed any more.

The harsh laws of the Polish state at the end of the war in 1945 meant that little remained of the once numerous, active and economically engaged community.

Hence the picture of the contribution of the German-Evangelical population, which lasted at least two centuries, is largely incomplete. If one looks at the records on the internet, we have only an insight into the period of the Second World War.

<!-- s. 324 [PDF 324] -->

There are only a few scholarly publications. I have come to the conviction that we will never create a more complete picture of the history of the town and the region unless we try to find out a little more about those who lived here for more than two hundred years and whose traces time has erased.

Despite the whole tragic period of the years 1939-1945, it should be borne in mind that it is only one part of the history of this land and its inhabitants that one should attempt to tell. It is a broken link in the chain of mutual relations which formed a peculiar code of life and an image of those generations, of this community that lived simultaneously together and apart.

It was in the year 1718 that fourteen German families arrived from Silesia, to whom the owner of the Trąbczyn estate leased a large area of woodland and wasteland for perpetual use, on which in time the village called Łazińsk came into being. The owners of the Trąbczyn estate, Paweł and Katarzyna Prusiński, allocated land for the first two settlements. These were the villages of Trąbczyńskie Luźne Stare Olędry and Trąbczyńskie Luźnie Olędry (today Łazińsk Pierwszy and Drugi).

The settlers were German farmers of the Evangelical confession. Evidently the experience with the new settlers was good, for the estate owner Prusiński had a further settlement founded. The newcomers took over the leased land under Dutch law. They were free people, with their own self-administration, with a cult of work and strict morals. In the years 1746-1789 the owners of the Łukom estate had further settlements founded. These are the present-day villages of Bukowe, Imielno, Huta Łukomska and Myszakówek.

Polish peasants too – Protestants and Catholics – settled there in isolated cases and sporadically. In this way, over time, a ring of numerous villages and settlements, inhabited almost entirely by Germans, came into being around Zagórów, such as: Olchowo, Tarszewo, 
<!-- s. 325 [PDF 325] -->
 Chrościki, Anielewo, Michalinów, Augustynów, Wrąbczynkowskie Holendry and others. Schools (Kantoratsschulen) and houses of prayer were built and cemeteries laid out. In 25 localities within the territory of the present-day parish of Zagórów, Germans of the Evangelical confession lived for over 200 years as the dominant group. They formed the Evangelical community in the town, which came into being as a filial in the year 1843 and in the year 1856 became an independent parish. The name "Olędrzy" [Hauländers] was used for a long time in the parish of Zagórów and referred not to the nationality of the settlers but to the law under which the villages were founded and relations with the estate owners were regulated. This law was brought to Poland by the Mennonites who had fled from the Netherlands for religious reasons.

In August 2020 I paid a visit to Łukasz Parus – the director of the Cultural Centre in Zagórów – and laid before him the considerations described above. He was fire and flame for this idea, and what was important: he knew who should be invited to collaborate. Nor did one need to convince Waldemar Wunsz, the pastor of the Evangelical-Augsburg parish in Konin, within whose territory the Zagórów Evangelical church lies, of this idea.

And so the idea of undertaking work on the preparation of a publication on the history of the Evangelical population (of the Germans and Poles) in this region was realised. A few weeks later, at a meeting in the former Kantorhaus at Konińska Street 4 in Zagórów, an editorial committee was set up which set itself the goal of bringing the whole undertaking to a successful conclusion.

After almost three years, extensive material in the form of articles, interviews, reminiscences, documents and photographs has been gathered and compiled into a book.

It is difficult to determine the character of this volume because of the variety of the material presented. The translated 
<!-- s. 326 [PDF 326] -->
 table of contents listed below contains a synthetic overview of the content of the completed work.

Should we succeed in preserving and telling even a small fragment of this two-hundred-year history, it may perhaps be easier to understand it. That the work we undertook proved necessary to the community of the town of Zagórów is borne witness to by the numerous, rather unusual events that accompanied us.

A notice appeared on the website of the Cultural Centre in Zagórów asking people to make information, or documents, photographs, testimonies and certificates in their possession, accessible to the editorial committee. This appeal met with a quick response, and very interesting source materials were made available to the Cultural Centre. The Gesellschaft der Freunde der Gemeinde Zagórów announced a collection among internet users in order to support the project financially.

A completely exceptional event, by contrast, was the concert of the musician Daniel Słowiński and his friends, organised by Pastor Waldemar Wunsz on 23 September 2023 in the Evangelical church in Zagórów, which was combined with a collection for the publication of the book. The church was well attended by listeners, who after the concert gave the artists and the organiser an enormous round of applause. The money collected by the Gesellschaft and during the concert supplements the funds designated for this purpose by the mayor of the town of Zagórów, Roman Kultermann.

Erich Kuhn, too, continued to show great interest in our work, and he likewise supported us financially. We highly value the fact that Dr. Zbigniew Chodyła, a merited researcher and connoisseur of the history of the German settlers (the so-called Hauländers) in Wielkopolska, stood by our side with scholarly advice in the realisation of our publication.

<!-- s. 327 [PDF 327] -->

We shall endeavour to name separately all the donors and sponsors thanks to whom the printing of the book will be possible.

This book is the result of a joint effort. Special thanks for the cooperation and the commitment go to all the authors. However, I regard it as my duty to single out in particular the contribution of Katarzyna Połom, Łukasz Parus, Damian Kruczkowski and Günter Hennings, as well as the contribution of the translators in the translations from the German language, and the commitment of Jacek Kin, who produced the greater part of the translations and made available a number of important materials. Without their outstanding achievement the publication could not have come into being.

> *Mirosław Słowiński*  
> *Translated by Jacek Kin*

### Acknowledgements

Acknowledgements

Work on the book about the Evangelicals of the Zagórów land met with lively interest and support from many people. The Towarzystwo Przyjaciół Ziemi Zagórowskiej announced a collection on the internet as part of the Pomagam.pl project, from which the funds obtained will be allocated to covering part of the costs of printing. We extend our heartfelt thanks to all the persons, including those who made their donations anonymously. The sum collected, 9910.00 zł, made it possible to close the budget and to carry the entire publishing process through safely.

<!-- s. 330 [PDF 330] -->

**On 23 September 2023, in the Evangelical-Augsburg Church** in Zagórów, an extraordinary concert took place: **Music and the Sacred**.

The originators of the event were Pastor Waldemar Wunsz and Daniel Słowiński, and the main aim was to support the printing of the book *The Broken Link. Evangelicals of the Zagórów Land*.

Performing: Daniel Słowiński and friends – Zuzanna Wilczyńska, Agnieszka Witkiewicz, Joanna Słowińska and Wojciech Nowakowski.

The patrons of the concert were the Fundacja Alana Woźniak Per Musicam and the MGOK in Zagórów.

Especially for this occasion Kinga Kujawska fired commemorative ceramic tiles with the image of the Zagórów Evangelical church.

The audience that filled the church thanked the performers with long standing ovations.

The donations collected in the collection box from the participants of the concert boosted the publishing budget by the sum of 3200 zł.

##### We Thank

To the translators: Lidia Antoniewicz, Aneta Gorzelańczyk, Anna Eicke, Jacek Kin, Jolanta Łuczak, Michał Rek, Sylwia Roszak and Günter Hennings, for preparing the materials from German sources and for the language consultations.

To Andrzej Bernat for opening wide his archive and helping to gather materials, to Ewa Kwiecień for making available the Chronicle of the Primary School in Wrąbczynkowskie Holendry, and to Barbara and Jerzy Leśniewski for donating the documents of the late Bogdan Grzonkowski.

To Erich Kuhn from Detmold and to the people gathered in the Freundeskreis Zagórów – Konin for their support and friendly cooperation.
